### To the laudable
# FRATERNITY
### of the Rose Cross, H. Ar. wishes

->Noble Rose of the Lion of the North <note>The "Lion of the North" was a popular prophetic figure in 17th-century Protestant circles, often associated with a coming savior or reformer from the North.</note>, Grace and Favor: and<-
->provided by God the Almighty, the only wise<-
->and kind, temporal and eternal<-
->well-being.<-

Since in the past year of 1615, I first heard the name of the Brothers of the Rose Cross, I held it to be a farcical trick <note>original: "Gauckelspiel" — a deceptive performance or juggling act</note> or Popish nonsense, and did not wish to inquire further about it. I have often heard since then that others have thought the same, and many of them still do, because within the <term>Papacy|The system of government and religious authority of the Roman Catholic Church</term> there have been many <term>Orders|Religious communities such as monks or friars</term> against the **LORD** Christ. Most recently, the Devil brought forth from the abyss of Hell the <term>Jesuwider|A derogatory Protestant pun on "Jesuit," literally meaning "Against-Jesus"</term> and the <term>Capuchins|A reform branch of the Franciscan friars</term>—such locusts, caterpillar-vermin, and brood of vipers—and I held this Brotherhood to be of the same kind. But when afterwards, while doing other things and not paying attention <note>original Latin: "aliud agens nee attendens"</note>, I was informed that they held the Jesuwiders and the Roman Pope to be the great Antichrist,

<summary>The author H. Ar. addresses the Rosicrucian Brotherhood, admitting that he initially dismissed them as a Catholic deception or a farce until learning of their anti-Papal stance.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's narrative of how he first encountered the Rosicrucian writings. It transitions from his initial suspicion that the group might be related to the Pope's "Antichrist" agents to his personal investigation of their literature following a debate with a theological rival.</meta>

I previously assumed that this Fraternity might name itself after the seal of Dr. Luther <note>The "Luther Rose" is the recognized symbol of Lutheranism, featuring a cross in a heart inside a rose.</note>, in which a cross is found within a heart, and the heart within a rose. However, I still considered it an Anabaptist <note>original: "Widertäufferische" — referring to radical Reformation movements that were often viewed as fanatical or dangerous by mainstream Protestants</note> fantasy, the kind of foolish chatter and monkey-business that appears annually in print and in filth. 

This was especially so when I recently had a debate <note>original Latin: "concertationem"</note> with an over-clever, newly-risen Calvinist. He was a disputer <margin>x</margin> without a conclusion <note>original Latin: "absque conclusione"</note>, "always learning and never coming to the knowledge of the truth" <note>original Latin: "semper discens & nunquam ad veritatis agnitionem perveniens," quoting 2 Timothy 3:7</note>, scurrilously mocking everyone’s everything, and hewing everything to the line without a line <note>original Latin: "omnia ad amussim absq; amussi dolans" — an idiom meaning he was hyper-critical or trying to be precise without having any actual standard</note>. Among his usual mockeries and subtleties, he also showed me this little tract, yet he hid it as something special coming from Kassel <note>The city of Kassel was a major center for early Rosicrucian activity and printing</note>, of which the <term>Ubiquitists|A derogatory name for Lutherans who believed that Christ’s physical body is present everywhere (ubiquitous), particularly in the Eucharist</term> were not worthy. In it, I then read a little of *The Reformation of the World* <note>original Latin: "De Reformatione Orbis"</note>, which is printed at the back. 

And because I had been fully informed by Holy Scripture regarding the Reformation—not that all vice, all injustice, all labor, and all evil should be removed from the world, but that in the three hierarchies of men, there should be a more tolerable and better state of the world than under all four beastly <note>The text cuts off mid-word ("Bestialis-") with the catchword "schen," likely referring to the "Four Beastly Monarchies" from the biblical Book of Daniel.</note>

<summary>The author describes his initial dismissal of the Rosicrucians as "Anabaptist fantasy" until a Calvinist opponent showed him a tract from Kassel. He begins to contemplate a biblical "Reformation of the World" that improves human society across its hierarchies.</summary>

<keywords>Luther Rose, Anabaptists, Calvinists, Ubiquitists, Kassel, De Reformatione Orbis, Three Hierarchies, Four Monarchies</keywords>

...of *Christ, or from their own works* <note>original Latin: "Christi, sive ex propriis operibus"</note>, of which they, however, claim none for themselves.

But when at last a copy from Augsburg <note>A major center for printing and banking in 17th-century Germany</note> was sent to me, I found it quite different from what I had imagined. Among other things, however, I noted four points. Concerning these, I wish to answer and judge as well as I understand, since you, *oh Brothers*, desire the *judgments of the learned* <note>original Latin: "doctorum Judicia"</note> and cannot escape those of the *unlearned*.

Firstly, concerning the *History of the Order, and its Profession of Christianity* <note>original Latin: "Historia Ordinis, ejusdemq; Professione Christianismi"</note>.

Thirdly, concerning several other future things.

<meta>This page continues the author’s theological argument, moving from his personal humility to a sharp critique of religious factions. [He appears] simple to the world: Mighty in His deeds / which appear small before the world: Kind and Merciful toward us poor human beings / even if He chastises us. And even though Satan sets himself up as a god of this world / and partly resists God / partly is God's ape <note>original: "Gottes Affe"; a common early modern concept (Simia Dei) where the Devil mimics God’s miracles or institutions to deceive the faithful</note> / and wants to disguise himself as an angel of light / he can nonetheless become like no god / so that / one would not easily recognize him. One might take his tools as an example / either the Sacramentarians <term>Sacramentarians: A derogatory term used by 17th-century Lutherans to describe those (like Calvinists) who believed the bread and wine of the Eucharist were merely symbols</term> or the Jesu-enemies <note>original: "Jesuwider," a derogatory pun on "Jesuit," replacing "-it" with "wider" (against), implying they are "Against-Jesus"</note>. For these Jesu-enemies belong to the **Western** Antichrist <note>A common Protestant designation for the Pope</note> / about which no one doubts / whom even many of the Antichrist's own servants / such as bishops and prelates, recognize as such. But the Sacramentarians and Calvinists / [the proof] that they belong to the **Eastern** [Antichrist] <note>Likely referring to the "Turkish Antichrist" (Islam), with whom polemicists often compared Calvinists due to their views on predestination and the nature of Christ</note> (*as the whole First Epistle of John bears witness.*)

<margin>
Islam <note>original: "Mahometismus"</note> is indeed outside of the Christian Church / but as much of it as weaves itself into the Church / is the same as Arianism <term>Arianism: An ancient heresy that taught Jesus was a created being, not fully divine and one with God</term> and Calvinism. In the same way, Paganism <note>original: "Ethnicismus"</note> has also woven itself into the Church, and the Papacy <note>original: "Babstum"</note> grew out of it.

shall be demonstrated at another time / when their spirit’s **sevenfold zeal for lying** <note>original Latin: "septuplex mentiendi studium"</note> will be clearly / proven and set forth.

<meta>This page continues the author’s polemic against rival Christian denominations. Having previously categorized Calvinists and Jesuits as tools of different "Antichrists," the author now moves to specific "lies" he attributes to their theology and history. He uses the Latin phrase "Hoc non est corpus meum" (This is NOT my body) to parody the Calvinist symbolic view of the Eucharist, contrasting it with the literal Lutheran view. The catchword "Calvi-" from the previous page leads directly into this section's title.</meta>

# The Lying Calvinist.

The reason / why this is discussed at length here / will be shown below / for I am well aware / that I will not convert them.

I. **In dogmas:**
When he proposes contradictions to the words of Scripture: such as, "This is **not** my body" <note>original Latin: "Hoc non est corpus meum"; the author is satirizing the Calvinist view of the Lord's Supper by adding "not" to Christ's words "This is my body"</note>: which all their disputations and lying figures of speech <term>Tropes: Rhetorical devices or metaphors</term> imply. Such as the Sacramentarian <term>Sacramentarians: A term used by Lutherans for those who denied the "Real Presence" of Christ's body in the bread and wine</term> metonymy <term>Metonymy: A figure of speech where one word is substituted for another it is associated with; Calvinists argued "body" was a metonym for "sign of the body"</term>: the *changing of names* <note>original Greek: "ἀλλοίωσις ὀνομάτων"</note>: the *Pierian trope of exchange* <note>original Latin: "τρόπος ἀντιδόσεως Pierianus"; likely a reference to a specific school of rhetoric</note>. Thus: "I am **not** with you until the end of the age" <note>A mocking reversal of Matthew 28:20</note>. "To me is **not** given all authority in heaven and on earth" <note>A mocking reversal of Matthew 28:18</note>. "In that he himself has suffered being tempted, he is **not** able to help those who are being tempted" <note>original Greek: "ἐν ᾧ πέπονθεν αὐτός πειρασθείς οὐ δύναται τοῖς πειραζομένοις βοηθῆσαι"; a satirical negation of Hebrews 2:18</note>. "Our Lord was **not** betrayed in the night" <note>original Latin: "Non Dominus noster in nocte tradebatur"</note>: And that which was betrayed, was **not**

<column-break/>

So now, when we consider the history of the Jesuit Order / we find no wondrous counsels of God among them / except that Canisius <note>Peter Canisius (1521–1597), a prominent Jesuit influential in German lands</note> drove out devils / and also brought them to the point / that they prayed not only poorly for themselves / but also for the Roman Church / which neither the Lord Christ nor the Apostles were able to do <note>The author uses heavy sarcasm here to suggest that the Jesuits’ specialized prayers for the Papacy are an unscriptural and modern invention</note>.

That even the Devil is not powerful through them / is obvious from this / that everything goes backward / and everything falls back on their own heads / whatever they arrange with the greatest diligence and power. One should consider here the Spanish Armada in the year 1588 <note>The great Spanish fleet defeated by England and a "Protestant Wind" (storms); Protestants cited this as proof that God opposed the Catholic/Jesuit cause</note>.

<summary>The author accuses Calvinists of effectively negating the Bible through their metaphorical interpretations and mocks the Jesuits by citing the failure of the Spanish Armada as proof of their lack of divine favor.</summary>
<keywords>Calvinism, Jesuits, Eucharist, Peter Canisius, Spanish Armada, polemic, tropes, Real Presence</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's polemical attack on the "Lying Calvinist" and the Jesuits. ...was our Lord.
"Come **not** unto me, all you who labor and are heavy laden" <note>A mocking reversal of Matthew 11:28</note>. It is **not** that he who does not believe is judged because he does not believe, but because it absolutely pleased God to have it so <note>The author is attacking the Calvinist doctrine of "absolute decree" or double predestination, suggesting they believe God damns people simply because He wants to, rather than because of their unbelief</note>. And thus all the interpretations <note>original Greek: διανοιας (*dianoias*)</note> and explanations of the Sacramentarians <term>Sacramentarians: A derogatory term for those who denied the physical presence of Christ's body in the bread and wine of the Eucharist</term> are contradictory to the first [principle].

II. **In defense.**
Here let someone consider how well they have learned that saying: "Slander boldly, for something always sticks" <note>original Latin: *calumniare audacter, semper aliquid adhæret*</note>. What mockery, what insults they have poured out upon our people! From what kind of insult have they abstained? What blasphemies against God and Christ have they not vomited forth? Which is yet called by them a "golden jewel" and a "golden ladder to heaven," and these must be deemed very necessary reports. What sophistries <term>Sophistries: Clever but false or misleading arguments</term> do they not use—

<column-break/>

—thus, the greater the danger they threaten us with, the greater the damage they themselves suffer. And when they think they have done the most harm, Christ yet triumphs in His witnesses, so that it is well said: "He who dwells in heaven laughs at them, and the Lord mocks them" <note>A reference to Psalm 2:4</note>.

But how kind and merciful God—or the Devil—is within them is shown by the Spanish Inquisition <term>Inquisition: A powerful office within the Catholic Church for rooting out heresy, often associated with torture and execution</term>, the Spanish expedition against England <note>The Spanish Armada of 1588, which attempted to overthrow the Protestant Queen Elizabeth I</note>, and various plots against Germany <note>original: *Deudtschland*</note>, where, to please the Pope <note>original: *Pabst*</note>, they bring ruin to land and people, and finally go straight to the Devil. They want [to convert] the people with sword and fire—

<summary>The author accuses his theological rivals of intentionally perverting the words of Christ and using political violence, such as the Spanish Inquisition and the Armada, to enforce their will. He frames their theological interpretations as sophisticated lies that ultimately contradict the core of the Gospel.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's polemical attack against his theological opponents (the Calvinists and Jesuits). The previous page ended with a discussion of the dangers and insults hurled by these groups. This page transitions into a critique of their interpretive methods, accusing them of twisting both Scripture and Philosophy to deceive the unlearned, and concludes with a condemnation of forced religious conversion.</meta>

...to pull the wool over the eyes of the unlearned? What frauds from Theology and Philosophy have they not busied themselves weaving together? Truth, it seems, is not sufficient for itself and has need of the trickery of lies. Let this be an example: "It is necessary for Christ to be contained in heaven." <note>This refers to the "extra calvinisticum," a doctrine where Reformed theologians argued that Christ’s human nature must remain locally in heaven and cannot be physically present in the bread and wine of the Eucharist</note> And "repentance" <note>original Greek: *metanoia*</note> in Hebrews 12: which in the stated place signifies a simple change of mind and a revocation of a promise in the case of Isaac attributing the right of primogeniture and the blessing to Jacob; but they themselves take it for the repentance of the sins of Esau in his conversion to God. <note>The author argues his opponents misinterpret Hebrews 12:17, which describes Esau finding "no place for a change of mind" in his father Isaac, by wrongly applying it to Esau's own spiritual repentance</note> They also labor to disturb and corrupt Philosophy itself, as long as they can fashion "kidnappings" <note>original Greek: *sylagōgias* — meaning to be carried off as legal spoil or captive</note> and "empty deceits" <note>original Greek: *kenas apatas*</note> according to the "tradition of men" and not according to Christ. <note>A reference to Colossians 2:8</note> From this, they deny and persist in denying what they themselves have written, no—

<column-break/>

—to force [others] into their religion, even though they can neither give the Holy Spirit for conversion, nor endure punishment or a Judge, nor be satisfied with the Word of God—which alone is the "hammer that breaks the rocks" <note>original Latin: *malleus comminuens Petras*, from Jeremiah 23:29</note> and the "sword penetrating to the division of soul and spirit, of joints and marrow" <note>original Latin: *gladius penetrans ad divisionem animae & Spiritus ossium & medullarum*, from Hebrews 4:12</note>, and which alone reveals the hearts and thoughts of men and converts them to God. Indeed, where Christ says: "Let the tares grow, lest perhaps you root up the wheat." <note>original Latin: *Sinite Zizania crescere, ne forte triticum evellatis*, from the Parable of the Tares in Matthew 13:29</note> These people must bring it to the point that John truly writes: "I saw the woman drunk with the blood—" <note>original Latin: *Vidi mulierem ebriam sanguine*, referencing the "Whore of Babylon" in Revelation 17:6, here used to accuse the author's opponents of being a persecuting, bloodthirsty church</note>

<summary>The author accuses his theological rivals of using deceptive philosophical and linguistic tricks to distort the Bible, specifically regarding the nature of Christ and the definition of repentance. He argues that true conversion comes through the Word of God alone, rather than the physical or political force used by his opponents.</summary>

The Mark of Brandenburg.

Let whoever wishes look at the recent acts. <note>Theodore</note> Beza himself testifies that nothing besides sincerity <note>original Latin: *candorem*</note> was lacking or present in those things which were required and always applied to distinguish light from darkness, truth from lies.

### III. In Propagation.

When they insinuate themselves into the courts and the republics of the cities, they deny and deny again that they approve of Calvin's dogma; they use our own phrases until they have grasped applause and the secular arm <note>meaning they wait until they have gained political power or the support of the ruling authorities</note>. The affairs of Meissen <note>original Latin: *Misnica* — referring to the region of Saxony</note> provide examples on several occasions. Truth, forsooth, has need of a lie. To the propagation and defense of Calvinism in Meissen once belonged the attack on Exorcism <note>Lutherans retained a minor rite of exorcism during baptism, which Calvinists sought to abolish as superstitious</note>. For the sincere teachers who understood the fraud and resisted were driven out, Calvinists were pushed in, and fraud was committed against the common people in a matter that was slippery in both directions; and the simple-minded were disturbed, so that many suffered the loss of truth. In short: *It was a business of the same pattern as when the Pharisees and Herod’s servants asked the LORD Christ: Master, is it right that we give the tax to Caesar, which God ordained for the Temple?* <note>The author is comparing his opponents' tactics to the trick question posed to Jesus in Matthew 22</note>

### IV. In profession.

They utterly deny that they are Calvinists or Sacramentarians <note>a derogatory term for those who believed the bread and wine of the Eucharist were merely symbols</note>: namely, so that no one may say: *I am of Paul, I of Apollos:* <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 1:12, where the Apostle Paul rebukes the church for dividing into factions</note> as if it were not even permitted for Christ

<summary>The author accuses Calvinist reformers of using political infiltration and linguistic deception to spread their doctrines, specifically citing conflicts over the rite of exorcism in Meissen. He portrays them as hypocrites who hide their true identity behind neutral language until they gain political power.</summary>
<keywords>communicatio idiomatum, Theodore Beza, Meissen, Exorcism, Sacramentarians, religious polemic, Brandenburg, ecclesiastical politics</keywords>

--- Page 19 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of theological opponents, specifically focusing on their perceived habit of concealing their true beliefs. The opening fragment "rit" completes the word "resisterit" (has resisted) from the previous page, referring to those who opposed the introduction of Calvinist ideas.</meta>

...[has resisted], it has become common to name them Nicolaitans <note>An early Christian sect mentioned in the Book of Revelation, here used as a generic term of abuse for heretics</note>. And for about 1300 years, Christians have erred by calling those heretics Arians <note>Followers of Arius, who taught that Jesus was not co-eternal with God the Father</note> who deny the deity of **the Word** <term>tou logou: the Greek term for "the Word," referring to the second person of the Trinity</term>. Even when they are ordered to publish their confession of faith, they are accustomed to covering their true opinion with every possible artifice; and this deceit is actually considered praiseworthy among them. Let that man Crenzehemius in Silesia be an example for all. 

And Pierius <note>Urbanus Pierius (1546–1616), a German theologian accused of "Crypto-Calvinism"</note> said: "It is better for me to be freed from prison by perjury than to waste away in prison as someone useless to the Church." We need not recount the various disguises used by others in publishing their confessions; we certainly praise the **Candor** of the Papists <note>A term used by Protestants to refer to Roman Catholics</note> in this regard, for they at least are open about their beliefs. Indeed, it is considered a matter of great skill to drag a Calvinist out from their hiding places into the light. 

For they know how to deceive handsomely, and a clever lie is considered fitting for them. Indeed, here belongs the deceptive concord of Bucer <note>Martin Bucer (1491–1551), a Reformer known for his attempts to find middle ground between different Protestant factions</note> which he established at Wittenberg. Likewise, Zwingli’s boasting that Luther had conceded to his side. To this belongs that whole "concord" so often demanded to be entered into with us, through which nothing but frauds are intended for us, and the truth is overwhelmed, following Bucer's example.

### V. In common life: 

Although they are the most proud and the most bitter of men, they pretend to be the most humble and the most gentle. I remember laughing once because many of my fellow students, despite being empty and void of all real learning, would incline their minds toward the Sacramentarians <note>A term for those who denied the "Real Presence" of Christ's body and blood in the bread and wine of the Eucharist</note> in the evening over their drinks; by the next morning, they were immediately being proclaimed as the most learned of men. Thus "one mule scratches another" <note>A Latin proverb (mulus mulum scabat) referring to mediocre people who praise one another to boost their own status</note>. 

Indeed, I preferred to be despised and censured than to be so vainly praised, and I thought it better to *be* something rather than merely to *seem* it. Hence, those **vanities** <note>original: "vanitates" — the text ends with the first part of this word, "vani-", matching the catchword "nitates" for the following page</note> are hateful to me.

<summary>The author accuses Calvinist theologians of habitual dishonesty, citing historical figures like Bucer and Pierius as examples of those who use "clever lies" to advance their cause. He also mocks the intellectual pretensions of his peers who adopted these views to gain unearned status.</summary>

<keywords>Nicolaitans, Arians, Urbanus Pierius, Martin Bucer, Calvinism, Wittenberg Concord, Sacramentarians, academic vanity, religious polemic</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's critique of Reformed (Calvinist) theologians, specifically completing a sentence about the "deceptive concord of Bucer" which refers to Martin Bucer's efforts to find a middle ground on the Eucharist, which the author views as dishonest.</meta>

...abilities, so that I observed them performing more difficult, disguised, crafty, and fraudulent deeds. But putting aside these vile things. To this pertains the fact that they make God the author of all evil. As **Beza** <note>Theodore Beza (1519–1605), the successor to Calvin in Geneva</note> says: 

> God does not only excite the evil will of a robber to kill another, but He even directs his hands and weapon.

**Calvin's Institutes** <note>The *Institutes of the Christian Religion*, the definitive work of John Calvin's theology</note>: 

> The impure spirit is called God’s Spirit because it responds to His nod and power, being His instrument rather than an author acting on its own.

It is shameful even to mention **Zwingli’s** bull <note>original: "taurum"; likely referring to a specific crude statement or theological blunder by Huldrych Zwingli, the Swiss Reformer</note>. But what does **Christ** say in **John 8**? 

> The devil was a murderer from the beginning (in Paradise) and did not stand in the truth, because there is no truth in him. When he speaks a lie, he speaks from his own nature, for he is a liar and the father of lies.

And the **General Epistle of John**: 

> If we say that we have <term>fellowship</term> <note>original: "κοινωνίαν" (koinōnian), meaning a close spiritual communion or participation</note> with God and walk in darkness, we lie, and the truth is not in us.

## VI. In history. 
Perhaps it is enough that they attempted to claim the **Augsburg Confession** <note>The primary statement of faith for the Lutheran Church, presented in 1530</note> for themselves, since their own confession was not accepted. But that is not enough: for they also most shamelessly lied, claiming that **Luther** and other upright men had gone over to the sect of the **Sacramentarians** <note>A term of reproach used for those who denied the Real Presence of Christ’s body and blood in the bread and wine of the Lord's Supper</note>. There was even one who would have said that **Zwingli** attacked the Papal trifles and indulgences before **Luther**, if he could have; for it would have been too shameless a lie. He therefore calls into doubt who first came forward against the Pope.

## VII. In political practice: 
Since their God is a liar and deceives men with both a hidden and a revealed will <note>A reference to the Calvinist distinction between God's "revealed will" in Scripture and His "secret decree," which critics argued made God appear duplicitous</note>, they have learned to busily imitate Him. As

Now, to deceive and trick is no longer a disgrace; breaking pacts and agreements is held as a point of praise. Indeed, they are not far from those enemies of truth who hold it as an article of their faith that **faith need not be kept with a heretic** <note>original Latin: "Hæretico fidem non servandam esse"; a common polemical accusation suggesting that opponents feel religiously justified in breaking promises to those they deem heretics</note>. From this arise so many protests contrary to their actual deeds—not to mention other examples in public political records. They harass and oppress the ministers of the Church of the **Augsburg Confession** <note>The defining statement of Lutheran faith, which the author is defending</note> secretly and craftily whenever they cannot do so openly, in order to intrude **Calvinists** into their places; they devote all their skill and effort to this. To this belongs the entire <term>guile</term> <note>original Greek: "πανουργία" (*panourgia*), meaning craftiness, trickery, or unscrupulous cleverness</note> of **Dr. Crell** <note>Nikolaus Krell (c. 1550–1601), Chancellor of Saxony, who was executed for his attempts to move the state toward Calvinism</note> and **Peucer** <note>Caspar Peucer (1525–1602), a physician and scholar imprisoned for decades for his "Philippist" or Crypto-Calvinist leanings</note>. This resulted in the proverb that "Calvinist faith and truth" is synonymous with a lie, and a "Calvinist coin" is called wicked and counterfeit. And I confess, if any hint of honesty had been observed in those men during the time of Peucer and Crell, all of **Meissen** <note>original Latin: "Misnia"; a historical region in Saxony</note> would have easily followed them; for it would have been less apparent that the Spirit of Truth is lacking in them.

Regarding the **Calvinists**, they are indeed strange in their schemes, being masters of making black out of white, and white out of black. But nevertheless, they cannot equal God; He is also "wonderful" <note>original German: "wunderlich"; used here to mean God works in mysterious or striking ways</note> within them, and can quite handily bring them to their **martyrdoms**. They certainly boast of these, but in a "good Calvinist" way; for although they boast of how much blood they have shed for the sake of their religion and the testimony of **Jesus Christ**, yet such Cal-

### VI. In history.

Now to deceive and trick is no longer a disgrace; breaking pacts and agreements is considered a matter of praise. Indeed, they are not far from those enemies of truth who hold as an article of their faith that "faith need not be kept with a heretic." <note>original Latin: "Hæretico fidem non ſervandam eſſe"</note> From this have arisen so many protests that are contrary to their actual deeds. And—not to mention other examples in public political records—they harass and oppress the ministers of the Church of the Augsburg Confession <note>The primary confession of faith for Lutherans, which the author is defending against Calvinist influence</note> secretly and cunningly, since they cannot do so openly, in order to push in Calvinists; they apply all their art and effort to this matter. To this belongs the entire cooperation <note>original Greek: "συνεργία" (*synergia*)</note> of **Dr. Crell** <note>Nikolaus Krell (c. 1550–1601), the Chancellor of Saxony who was executed for his attempts to replace Lutheranism with "Crypto-Calvinism"</note> and **Peucer** <note>Caspar Peucer (1525–1602), a scholar and son-in-law of Melanchthon who was imprisoned for his Calvinist leanings</note>, through which it has come about that, as the proverb goes, "Calvinist faith and truth" is used as a name for a "Lie," and a "Calvinist coin" is used to describe something wicked and counterfeit. And I confess, if any sliver of sincerity had been noticed in them during the time of Peucer and Crell, all of **Meissen** <note>original Latin: "Misniam"; a region in Saxony that was a focal point of these religious conflicts</note> would have easily followed them; for it would have been less obvious that the Spirit of truth fails them.

Concerning the Calvinists, they are indeed strange in their schemes, as they are masters at making black out of white and white out of black. But nonetheless, they cannot match God, who is also wondrous in them and can well lead them to their martyrdoms, of which they certainly boast, but in the "good Calvinist way." For although they boast of how they have shed much blood for the sake of their religion and the testimony of Jesus Christ, yet such Cal- <note>The text breaks off here to continue on the next page; the catchword indicates the word is "Calvinist"</note>

<meta>This page continues the Lutheran polemic against Calvinism. ...honor. Therefore, those same executed Lutherans are martyrs, even if they were otherwise labeled as Sacramentarians <note>original Latin: *etsi aliàs sacramentarii*. "Sacramentarians" was a derogatory term used by Lutherans for those who denied the physical presence of Christ in the bread and wine of the Lord's Supper.</note>. But the Calvinist martyrs—insofar as they are Calvinists—include, among others, Karlstadt, Oecolampadius, and Stössel, who were <term>self-condemned</term> <note>original Greek: *αυτοκατάκριτοι* (autokatakritoi); a biblical term for someone whose own actions or words provide the judgment against them.</note> and died in despair, without physical illness or delirium, because they knowingly and willingly <note>original Latin: *scientes volentes*</note> attacked the truth and majesty of Jesus Christ and his followers. 

Zwingli himself, during a tedious dispute <note>original Latin: *tædiosa disputatione*. This likely refers to the 1525 debate over the Mass in Zurich.</note>, had to have an advisor, of whom he could not remember whether he was black or white: he was foolishly persuaded <note>original Latin: *aniliter persuasus*; literally "persuaded like an old woman," a common period insult for superstitious belief.</note> that if the figure were white, it must have been an angel of light. Beza, though in good health, is a similar martyr, asserting that he and his own people lack only purity <note>original Latin: *candorem*</note>, as was recently cited.

<margin>
But no Lutheran has yet fallen into despair because he defended the majesty of Jesus Christ against the Sacramentarians. And if it were to happen—dear God help us—what a noise they would make about it!
</margin>

Thus we find among the Calvinists that the promoter of their sect is neither wonderful in counsel, nor mighty in deed, nor kind, nor merciful toward us humans; especially because they, by profession and with every effort <note>original Latin: *ex professo & omni conatu*</note>, [strive for] wis- <meta>catchword: sapien- (likely *sapientiam* / wisdom)</meta>

### 1 John 3

-> B v <-

Thus this **Fraternity** also establishes that a person’s 1. Religion, 2. Governance <term>Policey: In the 17th century, this referred to the general order, administration, and moral regulation of a community</term>, 3. Health, 4. Limbs, 5. Nature, 6. Language, 7. Whatever is against this, let that be error, falsification, and from the Devil, who alone is the first, the middle, and the final cause of worldly dissonance, blindness, and stupor. In contrast, the Calvinists teach: *The Impure Spirit is called the Spirit of God* <note>original Latin: "Spiritus Impurus Dei Spiritus vocatur"</note>, *because it responds to his will and power, being more his instrument than acting from itself* <note>original Latin: "quod ad nutum potentiamque ejus respondeat, instrumentum magis ipsius quā a se"</note>

<column-break/>

*Let us not love in word nor in tongue: but in deed and in truth* <note>original Latin: "Non diligamus verbo neq; lingva : sed opere & veritate" — a quote from 1 John 3:18</note>. Much less will this way of speaking—which is opposed to deed and truth, and customary for the Calvinists—be in accordance with God and the Holy Scripture. And from whom will have come the **exchange of names** <term>ἀλλοίωσις ὀνομάτων (alloiosis onomaton): a rhetorical device used by some reformers to argue that attributes of Christ's divinity were merely attributed to his humanity in name only</term>, the **verbal communion** <term>κοινωνία (koinonia) verbalis: the theory that the divine and human natures of Christ share properties only in language, not in reality</term>, and the **sacramental metonymy** <term>metonymia sacramentaria: a figure of speech where the sign (bread/wine) stands for the thing signified (body/blood), used here as a criticism of non-literal views of the Eucharist</term>? And are these **proofs** <term>τεκμήρια (tekmeria)</term> of truth or of lies? And for what purpose were they invented? Unless it is so that a contradiction might be substituted for **the literal word** <term>τῷ ῥητῷ (to rheto)</term>. And in this they work *to earn a reward* <note>original Latin: "pro præmio promerendo"</note>. But if one takes two of these arts together, such as the **verbal communion** <term>κοινωνία (koinonia)</term> for the **exchange of names** <term>ἀλλοίωσις ὀνομάτων (alloiosis onomaton)</term>, for example in the saying: *Our Lord Jesus Christ, in the night he was betrayed* <note>original Latin: "Dominus noster Iesus Christus in qua nocte tradebatur"</note>; or *It is necessary for Christ [to be received by] heaven* <note>original Latin: "oportet Christum coelo [capi]" — The sentence ends mid-thought; the catchword "capi" on the original page suggests the completion of the Latin phrase meaning Christ must remain in heaven until the end of time</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's defense of his philosophical and theological framework. This history may be read by whoever wishes, also in the *Collections of Wolf* <note>Johann Wolf (1537–1600), a German jurist and historian famous for his "Lectionum memorabilium et reconditarum centenarii," a compilation of marvels and history.</note>, in the *Leisure Hours of Camerarius* <note>Joachim Camerarius the Younger (1534–1598), a physician and botanist who wrote "Symbola et Emblemata."</note>, and elsewhere.

The Abbot of Ursberg <note>Conrad of Ursberg (d. 1240), a medieval German chronicler.</note> reports something similar:
In the first year of Constantine and Irene <note>Constantine VI and his mother Irene, rulers of the Byzantine Empire (r. 780–797 AD).</note>, a certain man digging in the long walls of Thrace found a stone chest. When he had cleaned and opened it, he found a man lying in it and letters skillfully joined together, containing these words: **CHRIST SHALL BE BORN of the Virgin Mary, and I believe in him**. Under the Emperors Constantine and Irene, he added: **O Sun, you shall see me again**. This occurred in the year of Christ 773.

A similar story is read as having happened in the year 1237. For when a Jew in Toledo, a city in Spain, was preparing to split a rock near a village which overhung a vineyard—in order to enlarge the vineyard and let in the sun's rays—once the rock was split with wedges, he found a vaulted cavity, completely full...

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the divine and philosophical order of things, moving from the history of alphabets to the metaphysical distinction between good and evil. It is certainly true that if, by a **mental abstraction** <note>original Greek and Latin: "ἀφαίρεσει mentis"; a philosophical process of separating concepts in the mind to better understand their individual natures</note>, evil is drawn away from the good, then the good is found to reside in a beautiful order. Even evil cannot then be found without order, since it is arranged by GOD, and the devils maintain their order **by their own impulse** <note>original: "suopte motu"</note>; so that, even though they are **deprived of all good** <note>original: "privati omni bono"</note>, no one in their kingdom is disobedient to their prince, nor is any one against another, so that their kingdom may stand, as Christ himself shows <note>A reference to the Gospel of Matthew 12:26: "If Satan casts out Satan, he is divided against himself. Year / called the **Vacuum** <note>original Latin: "Vacuum"; literally "void" or "emptiness." <meta>This page continues the author's critique of theological and philosophical inconsistencies, specifically targeting Calvinist and Sacramentarian doctrines regarding the nature of truth and the will of God.</meta>

It has long been a source of annoyance and disgust to me that many are of the opinion that something can be true in **Theology**, but false in **Philosophy**; and conversely (*et contra*), true in **Philosophy**, and false in **Theology**. For because truth is unified and eternal, and agrees with itself in all places, such a thing simply cannot be possible. **Neither do the sciences nor the arts wage war against each other; why then should they contend as demons do in war?** <note>original Latin: "Neque scientiæ invicem aut artes belligerantur, cur Diaboli invicem bello non decertent."</note>

Yet nevertheless, the entire dispute of the **Calvinists** concerning **Providence & Predestination** is of this kind, where they imagine **contraries and contradictions within God himself**, such as a **hidden and a revealed will** <note>original Latin: "voluntatem occultam & revelatam". This refers to the theological idea that God's public promises might differ from His secret decrees.</note>—a concept that would be considered shameful even among men. And **Paul**, where he warns us against the deception of **Philosophy**—and specifically <note>original: "specifice"</note> in that same passage he refers to the **Sacramentarians** <term>Sacramentarians: a derogatory term used by Lutherans to describe those, such as Calvinists or Zwinglians, who denied the physical presence of Christ's body in the Bread and Wine of the Eucharist</term>, and indeed this **warning of Paul** <note>original: "admonitio Pauli"</note> cannot be more fittingly directed against any **heretics** in the entire history of the Church, because of the **Subject and Predicate** <note>Technical terms from logic referring to the components of a proposition or argument</note> he has before him—he calls it

<margin>
**John**, in his **Epistle**, calls it **"speaking from the world."** <note>original Greek: ἐκ τοῦ κόσμου λαλεῖν</note>
</margin>
**plunder through philosophy and empty deceit, according to the tradition of men, according to the basic principles of the world, and not according to Christ.** <note>original Greek: συλαγωγίας διὰ τῆς φιλοσοφίας καὶ κενῆς ἀπάτης, κατὰ τὴν παράδοσιν τῶν ἀνθρώπων, κατὰ στοιχεῖα τοῦ κόσμου, καὶ οὐ κατὰ Χριστόν. This is a direct quotation of Colossians 2:8.</note> At which point the **Socinians**... <note>The text breaks off; "Soc." likely refers to the Socinians, a rationalist Christian movement that denied the Trinity.</note>

<meta>This page continues the author's polemic against "Sacramentarian" or Calvinist philosophical errors, transitioning into a discussion on the nature of alchemy—specifically the "Art of the Stone"—and how the fundamental principles of natural propagation are misunderstood by both the greedy and the supposedly learned.</meta>

...theological preceptors elegantly and diligently expounded and thoroughly explained these **Calvinist falsifications and corruptions of philosophy** to us.

It is rightly and well said that the ***artifice of the stone*** <note>original Latin: "artificium lapidis," the alchemical process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> is a ***by-product*** <note>original Latin: "parergum"</note> of ***philosophy*** (***not to say of a Christian man***); for not only is the ***preparation*** <note>original Latin: "confectio"</note> ***women's work and children's play*** <note>original Latin: "opus mulierum & lusus puerorum," a common alchemical phrase suggesting that the physical work is easy once the secret theory is understood</note>, and easier to learn than making a pair of shoes: but the ***investigation*** <note>original Latin: "indagatio"</note> is also neither the most noble nor the most beautiful, nor the darkest or most subtle (except that God Himself has hidden it for the sake of the wicked and for the sake of worldly order), since all seven ***perfectly mixed bodies*** <note>original Latin: "corpora perfectè mixta," the seven traditional metals: gold, silver, mercury, copper, iron, tin, and lead</note> have their ***propagation*** <note>original Latin: "propagationem"</note> ***in their own kind*** <note>original Latin: "suo genere"</note>. This is true even though the Lapps and Sami people <note>original: "Lappenländer vnd Sameiden," indigenous peoples of Northern Scandinavia</note> know and understand the ***propagation and multiplication of grain*** <note>original Latin: "farris"</note> as little as those who cannot sleep at night for ***gold-fever*** <note>original German: "Goldsucht," implying a pathological greed for wealth</note> understand the ***propagation of metals*** <note>original Latin: "propagationem metallorum"</note>. Indeed, if one were to truly ***philosophize***, the smallest part of those who consider themselves highly learned would understand the ***propagation*** of either humans, or animals, or herbs, or stones, or ***Sulfurs***, or ***Salts***, or the Elements, for—

<margin>The LORD will not leave him unpunished who misuses His Name.</margin>
Long ago, I detested and cursed not only the blasphemous <note>original German: "Gotteslesterlichen"</note> imagery and parables of many <term>Alchemists: early practitioners of chemistry and philosophy who sought to transform matter and achieve spiritual perfection</term>, and the abominable misuse of God's Name for the sake of ***gold-making***; all of whom, as is certain, know nothing themselves of this art which they wish to teach others: ***For wisdom does not enter into a malicious soul*** <note>original Latin: "Neque enim cadit sapientia in animam malignam"</note>. For this reason, in my youth I was not only an enemy to the entire art, but even today, in my whole library, I cannot tolerate more than three or four authors in this art or faculty.

One also hears with amazement how adventurously <note>original German: "Ebenthëwerlich," meaning in a strange, fantastical, or storytelling manner</note> many an individual attempts to hide his ***pride*** or ***greed*** <note>original Latin: "superbiam aut avaritiam"</note>:

## A Supplemental Stumbling Block. <note>original Greek: "Πάρεργον σκάνδαλον" (Parergon skandalon). A "parergon" is an additional work or byproduct of a main task; a "skandalon" is a stumbling block or a point of contention.</note>

Because the **Sacramentarians** <term>Sacramentarians: A term used during the Reformation (primarily by Lutherans) to describe theologians like Zwingli or Calvin who believed the bread and wine in the Eucharist were symbols rather than the literal body and blood of Christ.</term> say: How can this man give us his flesh to eat and his blood to drink <note>A reference to the questions posed by the crowds in John 6:52.</note>, since he has nevertheless ascended into heaven and is not here, only to return from there at the **final day** <term>final day: The Day of Judgment or the end of the world.</term>; and thus his body and his entire humanity are separated from us in the **Eucharistic assembly** <term>Eucharistic assembly: Latin "synaxis", a term for the gathering of the faithful for Holy Communion.</term> by as great a distance as the furthest heaven is from the deepest earth. Necessarily, then, those words—"This is my body" and "He ascended into heaven"—must be interpreted in such a way that the reality of Christ’s human nature is not destroyed. <note>The author is summarizing the "Sacramentarian" argument: if Christ is truly human, his body must be in one place (heaven) and cannot be physically present in the bread and wine on earth.</note>

<summary>The author begins a theological supplement addressing the "Sacramentarian" view of the Eucharist, which argues that Christ's physical body remains in heaven and cannot be present in the bread and wine. This reflects the intense 16th and 17th-century debates over the "Real Presence" and the nature of Christ's humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Sacramentarians, Eucharist, Real Presence, Ascension, Human Nature of Christ, Synaxis, Theology, Christology</keywords>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>The text continues a mathematical and cosmological calculation, likely from a treatise on the dimensions of the universe or a theological critique of contemporary worldviews. The catchword "go e-" suggests the next page begins with "go e[rgo]" or a similar construction.</meta>

If the diameter of a certain star of the first magnitude is to the Earth's diameter as 19 is to 4, then the diameter of **Spica** in Virgo, or the **Heart of the Lion** <note>original: "cordis leonis," the star known today as Regulus</note>, or the **Eye of the Bull** <note>original: "oculi tauri," the star known today as Aldebaran</note> will be 9 1/2 Earth radii, and this is the thickness of the **eighth sphere** <note>In the geocentric model, the eighth sphere was the firmament where the fixed stars were located.</note>. The **First Mover** <term>Primum Mobile</term> <note>The outermost moving sphere in traditional astronomy that carried all the inner spheres with it.</note> will not be any thinner than this; let it therefore be of the same thickness. And from here begins the **Empyrean Heaven** <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the heavens, often described as a place of pure light or fire and the abode of God.</note> according to the tradition of the **Catholics** <note>original: "Papistarum"</note> and **Calvinists** (whether it rests or moves, a "heaven-walking" <note>original Greek: οὐρανοβάτης (ouranobatēs)</note> sacramental faith would know). This heaven will be at a distance from the **Mount of Olives** <note>Likely used here as a symbolic or literal starting point on Earth, referencing the site of the Ascension.</note> of 10,050 1/4 minus 1/4 <note>meaning exactly 10,050</note> total Earth diameters, each of which is worth more than 1,718 **German miles** <note>A historical unit of distance; one German mile was approximately 4.6 modern English miles or 7.5 kilometers.</note>. So that we may consider ourselves warned in a timely and skillful manner, we shall walk at an equal pace with them, and in the same matter we will follow the same <gloss>method of knowledge, speaking from the physical world</gloss> <note>original Greek: ἐπιστήμης τρόπον ἐκ τοῦ κόσμου λαλοῦντες (epistēmēs tropon ek tou kosmou lalountes)</note>. Therefore, how many **German miles** <note>A historical unit of distance; one German mile was approximately 4.6 modern English miles.</note> is the **Empyrean Heaven** <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the heavens, often described as the abode of God.</note> distant from the earth? How great a journey could **Christ** complete daily, so that the truth of his human nature is not destroyed? <note>This is a theological argument: if Christ traveled as a physical human to a distant physical heaven, the speed required might be so great it would destroy a human body.</note> How far could Christ project his voice from heaven without it breaking, so that **Saul** might hear it on the road to **Damascus**?

For **Peucer** <note>Caspar Peucer (1525–1602), a famous German scholar and physician who wrote a standard textbook on the spheres of the universe.</note> reports in his little book on the Spheres that the **eighth sphere** <note>In the geocentric model, the sphere containing the fixed stars.</note> is distant from the earth on all sides by 20,081 and 1/28 **Earth radii**. Let the diameter of a certain star of the **first magnitude** <note>The brightest category of stars in the ancient and early modern classification system.</note> be to the **Earth's Diameter**, as 19 is to 4. Therefore, the diameter of **Spica** <note>original Latin: spicæ virginis; the brightest star in the constellation Virgo.</note>, or the **Lion's Heart** <note>original Latin: cordis leonis; the star Regulus.</note>, or the **Bull's Eye** <note>original Latin: oculi tauri; the star Aldebaran.</note> will be 9 1/2 **Earth radii** <note>original Latin: semidiametrorum Terræ</note>, and this is the thickness of the **eighth sphere** <note>In the geocentric model, the celestial sphere containing the fixed stars.</note>. The **Primum Mobile** <note>The "First Moved" sphere, the outermost moving sphere in the cosmos which was thought to drive the motion of all others.</note> will now not be thinner. Let it, therefore, be of the same thickness. And from here will begin the **Empyrean Heaven** <note>The highest, most spiritual part of the heavens, described by theologians as the dwelling place of God.</note> according to the tradition of the **Papists** <note>A term, often polemical, for Roman Catholics.</note> and **Calvinists** (whether it rests or moves, the **sky-treading** <term>sky-treader <note>original Greek: οὐρανοβάτης (ouranobates); referring to one who walks or dwells in the heavens.</note></term> sacramental faith might know), which will be distant from the **Mount of Olives** by 10,050 and 1/4 minus 1/4 <note>This likely indicates a calculation resulting in exactly 10,050 whole diameters, or perhaps reflects a specific astronomical notation of the era.</note> whole **Earth diameters**, each of which is worth more than 1,718 **German miles** <note>A historical unit of distance; one German mile was approximately 4.6 modern English miles.</note>.
